v_{k}^{\prime\prime}\in N^{\alpha_{k}}
and
g\in D
(since by Lemma
11.17
, the corresponding subalgebras form a splitting of
\operatorname{Lie}(G_{\alpha})
). In the first case when
w=e
, we set
w^{\prime}=e
. Furthermore, notice that
q_{2}
and the terms appearing before
q_{2}
all belong to
\Delta_{2}
, so we may combine them to reduce the expression to:

u_{n}^{\prime}*\dots*u_{k+1}^{\prime}*(q_{3})^{\prime}*w^{\prime}*v_{k}^{\prime\prime}*g*q_{4}*v_{k-1}*\dots*v_{1}*\rho^{0}

for some collection of
u_{i}^{\prime}\in N^{\alpha_{i}}
, and
g\in D
. But by Lemma
13.8
,
g
may be pushed to the right preserving the form of the expression and being absorbed into
\rho^{0}
. We abusively do not change these terms and drop
g
from the expression.

Now, we do the final commutation by commuting
v_{k}^{\prime\prime}
and
q_{4}
. Notice that
\left\{\alpha_{k}\right\}\cup\Delta_{1}\cup\Delta_{4}
is a stable subset. Therefore, we may write
v_{k}^{\prime\prime}*q_{4}
as
(q_{4})^{\prime}*v_{k}^{\prime\prime\prime}*q_{1}
with
q_{1}\in G_{\Delta_{1}},(q_{4})^{\prime}\in G_{\Delta_{4}}
and
v_{k}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in N^{\alpha_{k}}
. Inserting this into the previous expression, we see that the
q_{1}
term can be absorbed into the remaining product of the
v_{i}
terms. This yields the desired form

u_{n}^{\prime}*\dots*u_{k+1}^{\prime}*(q_{3})^{\prime}*w^{\prime}*(q_{4})^{\prime}*v_{k}^{\prime\prime\prime}*(q_{1}*v_{k-1}*\dots*v_{1})*\rho^{0},

with the new
\rho^{-}
equal to
(q_{3})^{\prime}*w^{\prime}*(q_{4})^{\prime}
.
∎

Recall that
D
commutes with the
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-action. We wish to combine the Lie groups
D
and
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K
to build a single Lie group to parameterize the neutral directions. We again consider the free product of groups
D*(\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K)
. Observe that
\mathbb{R}^{k}
commutes with the elements of this group, and
\mathbb{R}^{k}
has a dense orbit. Therefore, the cycles of this group at every point contain a co-finite dimensional normal subgroup (the cycle group at a point with a dense orbit). Let
\hat{D}
denote the factor of
D*(\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K)
by this group, and
\mathcal{C}_{\hat{D}}
the cycles in the group
D*(\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K)
.

Let
\hat{G}
be the quotient of
\hat{\mathcal{P}}:=(\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K)\ltimes\mathcal{P}
(see Definition
6.5
) by the smallest closed normal subgroup containing all elements from
\ker(\mathcal{P}\to G)
(ie, the relations already constructed in
G
) and elements of
\mathcal{C}_{\hat{D}}
.

Remark 13.10
.

Even in the case when the action satisfies
(HA-7)
(a)
, it is not necessarily true that the action of
\mathcal{P}
is transitive without saturating by
K
-orbits. The situation is even worse for abstract Anosov actions satisfying
(HA-7)
(b)
, in which one must saturate by both
K
and
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-orbits. Thus, to obtain a transitive action of a Lie group (and hence a homogeneous space structure), we must consider the groups
\hat{\mathcal{P}}
. Note also that including the subgroup
\mathbb{R}^{k}
in the homogeneous space structure makes it clear that the
\mathbb{R}^{k}
action is by translations in the homogenous coordinates.

Recall that
\mathcal{P}
is the free product of nilpotent Lie groups, and has a canonical CW-complex structure as described in Section
6.1
. The cell structure can be seen by considering subcomplexes corresponding to sequences of coarse weights
\bar{\chi}=(\chi_{1},\dots,\chi_{n})
and letting
C_{\bar{\chi}}=\left\{u_{1}*\dots*u_{n}:u_{i}\in N^{\chi_{i}}\right\}\cong N^{\chi_{1}}\times\dots\times N^{\chi_{n}}
. Then a neighborhood of the identity is a union of neighborhoods in each cell
C_{\bar{\chi}}
containing 0.

Let
\pi:\hat{\mathcal{P}}\to\hat{G}
denote the canonical projection, and note that
\ker\pi
is exactly the group generated by (conjugates of) commutator cycles, nontrivial symplectic cycles, cycles in
\mathcal{C}_{D}
, and cycles in
\mathcal{C}_{\hat{D}}
. Let
\pi^{\prime}:G_{+}\times G_{-}\times\hat{D}\to\hat{G}
denote the map
\pi^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},d)=g_{1}g_{2}d
, where
G_{+}
,
G_{-}
and
\hat{D}
are all identified with their inclusions into
\hat{G}
.

Proposition 13.11
.

There exists an open neighborhood
U
of
e\in\hat{\mathcal{P}}
and a continuous, open map
\Phi:U\to G_{+}\times G_{-}\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{D}}
such that
\pi\circ\Phi=\pi^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We describe the map
\Phi
, whose domain will become clear from the definition. Let
\Delta^{+}(a_{0})=\left\{\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n}\right\}
and
\Delta^{-}(a_{0})=\left\{\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{m}\right\}
be the coarse weights as described in the proof of Lemma
13.9
. Given a word
\rho=u_{1}*\dots*u_{n}
,
u_{i}\in N^{\chi_{i}}
,
\chi_{i}\in\Delta
for every
i
, we begin by taking all occurrences of
\alpha_{n}
in
\rho
and pushing them to the left, starting with the leftmost term. When we commute it past another
\alpha_{i}
, we accumulate only other
\alpha_{j}
,
i+1\leq j\leq n-1
, in
\rho^{\alpha_{i},\alpha_{n}}
, which we may canonically present in increasing order on the right of the commutation. A similar statement holds for the commutation of
\alpha_{n}
with
\beta_{i}
. We iterate this procedure as in the proof of Lemma
13.9
to obtain the desired presentation. Since the commutation operations involved are determined by the combinatorial type, the resulting presentation is continuous from the cell
C_{\bar{\chi}}
.

We now show that it descends to
\mathcal{P}
, which requires showing it respects the free product relations. If one of the terms happens to be
e
, the procedure yields the same result whether it is considered there or not. Furthermore, if a coarse weight is repeated, then commuting past each will yield elements contained in a single stable which may be combined afterwards. Thus, it is a well-defined continuous map from a neighborhood of the identity in
\hat{\mathcal{P}}
to
G_{+}\times G_{-}\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{D}}
. It is continuous from
\hat{\mathcal{P}}
because it is continuous from each
C_{\bar{\chi}}
.

Notice that in the application of Lemma
13.9
, we require that all terms are sufficiently small. Thus, in each combinatorial pattern, since the algorithm is guaranteed to have a finite number of steps and swaps appearing, and each term appearing will depend continuously on the initial values of the terms, we know that for each
\bar{\chi}
, some neighborhood of 0 will be in the domain of
\Phi
, by the neighborhood structure described above.

Notice that the reduction of a word
u
to a word of the form
u_{+}*u_{-}*a\in G_{+}\times G_{-}\times\hat{D}
uses only relations defining
\hat{G}
. Therefore, if after the reductions, the same form is obtained, the original words must represent the same element of
\hat{G}
. That is,
\pi\circ\Phi=\pi^{\prime}
.
∎

Corollary 13.12
.

The group
\hat{G}
is a Lie group.

Proof.

Choose
U
is as in Proposition
13.11
, and let
K\subset\Phi(U)
be a compact neighborhood of
(e,e,e)\in G_{+}\times G_{-}\times\hat{D}
. Note that such a neighborhood exists since
G_{\pm}
and
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K
are all Lie groups using Lemma
13.3
. Then
\Phi^{-1}(K)
is a neighborhood of
e\in\hat{\mathcal{P}}
, and
\pi(\Phi^{-1}(K))=\pi^{\prime}(K)
is a neighborhood of
e\in\hat{G}
. Therfore,
\hat{G}
is locally compact. Furthermore, since
\hat{G}
is the factor of the locally path-connected group
\hat{\mathcal{P}}
,
\hat{G}
is locally path-connected. Hence
\hat{G}
is a projective limit of Lie groups by Corollary
6.10
. Hence
\hat{G}
has an associated sequence
G_{n}
of connected Lie groups, factor maps
q_{n}:\hat{G}\to G_{n}
, and projections
p_{n}:G_{n}\to G_{n-1}
such that
\ker p_{n}
is compact,
q_{n}=p_{n+1}\circ q_{n+1}
, and
\bigcap_{n=1}^{\infty}\ker q_{n}=\left\{e\right\}
(see the diagram in Corollary
6.10
).

We first claim that there exists
N,d\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\dim(G_{n})=d
for all
n\geq N
. Indeed, note that since each
p_{n}
is surjective,
\dim(G_{n})\geq\dim(G_{n-1})
, so it suffices to show that
\dim(G_{n})\leq d
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and some
d\in\mathbb{N}
. We have that
q_{n}(G_{+})
,
q_{n}(G_{-})
and
q_{n}(\hat{D})
are Lie subgroups of
G_{n}
, and since
q_{n}\circ\pi\circ\Phi:G_{+}\times G_{-}\times\hat{D}\to G_{n}
is an open map, we conclude that the map
(g_{1},g_{2},a)\mapsto g_{1}\cdot g_{2}\cdot a
from
q_{n}(G_{+})\times q_{n}(G_{-})\times(\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K)
is an open map. It follows that
\dim(G_{n})\leq\dim(q_{n}(G_{+}))+\dim(q_{n}(G_{-}))+\dim(q_{n}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K))\leq\dim(G_{+})+\dim(G_{-})+\dim(\hat{D})
, which is independent of
n
.

Since
\dim(G_{n})=\dim(G_{n+1})
for all
n\geq N
,
p_{n}
is a local isomorphism for all
n\geq N+1
. It follows that the algebras
\operatorname{Lie}(G_{n})
are all isomorphic, and there exists a unique connected, simply connected group
\tilde{G}
such that
\operatorname{Lie}(\tilde{G})\cong\operatorname{Lie}(G_{n})
for sufficiently large
n
. We may therefore construct local isomorphisms
f_{n}:\tilde{G}\to G_{n}
inductively by defining
f_{n+1}
to be the unique Lie group homomorphism with derivative
(dp_{n})^{-1}\circ df_{n}
. We therefore obtain the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{n-2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{n-2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n-2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}

By the universal property of inverse limits, there exists a unique homomorphism
F:\tilde{G}\to\hat{G}
such that
q_{n}\circ F=f_{n}
. We claim that the image of
F
is exactly the path component of
\hat{G}
. Indeed, if
\gamma:[0,1]\to\hat{G}
is any path such that
\gamma(0)=e
, then
\gamma_{n}=q_{n}\circ\gamma
is a path in
G_{n}
, and
p_{n}\circ\gamma_{n}=\gamma_{n-1}
. Since
f_{n}
is a local isomorphism, there exists a unique
\tilde{\gamma}_{n}:[0,1]\to\tilde{G}
such that
f_{n}\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{n}=\gamma_{n}
. Since
p_{n}\circ f_{n}=f_{n-1}
, the maps
\tilde{\gamma}_{n}
all coincide, let
\tilde{\gamma}:[0,1]\to\tilde{G}
denote the corresponding lift. Then by construction,

q_{n}\circ F\circ\tilde{\gamma}=f_{n}\circ\tilde{\gamma}=\gamma_{n}.

Since
\gamma
is determined by the family of paths
\gamma_{n}
, it follows that
F\circ\tilde{\gamma}=\gamma
, and the endpoint of
\gamma
can be reached in the image of
F
.

Finally, since
\hat{G}
is path connected, the path identity component is exactly
\hat{G}
, so
F
is onto. Therefore,
\hat{G}
is the continuous image of a Lie group, and therefore Lie.
∎

13.2.
Description of the homogeneous spaces

By Corollary
13.12
,
X
is the homogeneous space of a Lie group
\hat{G}
, which is generated by subgroups which are images of
N^{\alpha}
,
\alpha\in\Delta
and
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K
. Furthermore, the group
\hat{G}
is a factor of the group
\hat{\mathcal{P}}=(\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K)\ltimes\mathcal{P}
, where
\mathcal{P}
is the free product of the groups
N^{\alpha}
, and let
\mathcal{C}
denote the kernel of
\hat{\mathcal{P}}\to\hat{G}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{C}
is the normal closure of the group generated by commutator relations
\rho^{\alpha,\beta}(u,v,x)
(which do not depend
x
), symplectic relations, and identifications of the diagonal elements of
G_{\alpha}
with the
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-action.

Let
S(x)=\operatorname{Stab}_{\hat{G}}(x)^{\circ}
, and notice that
S(x)
is the closed subgroup of a Lie group and therefore Lie.

Lemma 13.13
.

\dim(S(x))
is independent of
x\in X
.

Proof.

Since
\hat{G}
acts transitively on
X
by
(HA-7)
, it follows that
X
is the homogeneous space of the Lie group
\hat{G}
. The result is now immediate since if
g\in\hat{G}
,
S(x)
and
S(g\cdot x)
are subgroups conjugated by
g
.
∎

Let
a_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
be a generic element (ie, an element such that
\alpha(a_{0})\not=0
for all
\alpha\in\Delta
and
c_{i}\alpha(a_{0})\not=c_{j}\beta(a_{0})
for all functionals
c_{i}\alpha\in[\alpha]
,
c_{j}\beta\in[\beta]
.

Lemma 13.14
.

If
x
has a dense
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-orbit and is
a_{0}^{\pm 1}
-recurrent, and
S(x)\not=\left\{e\right\}
, then
\operatorname{Lie}(S(x))
contains an element of an Oseledets subspace of
\operatorname{Lie}(N^{\alpha})
for some
\alpha\in\Delta
.

Proof.

Suppose that
S(x)\not=\left\{e\right\}
, and consider
\operatorname{Lie}(S(x))\subset\operatorname{Lie}(G)
. Notice that
x\mapsto\operatorname{Lie}(S(x))
is semi-continuous in the following sense: if
x_{n}\to x
and
\mathbb{R}v_{n}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(S(x_{n}))
, with
\left|\left|v_{n}\right|\right|=1
and
v_{n}\to v
, then
\mathbb{R}v\subset\operatorname{Lie}(S(x))
.

Now, simply notice that if
S(x)\not=\left\{e\right\}
and
{a_{0}}^{n}\cdot x\to x
, then
S(x)
contains its fastest Oseledets space (either forward or backward), or is contained in
(\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K)
.
S(x)
is transverse to
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K
since the action of
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K
is locally free by the definition of a HAPHA. Therefore,
S(x)
contains an Oseledets space if it is nontrivial.
∎

Corollary 13.15
.

S(x)=\left\{e\right\}
for all
x\in X
.

Proof.

If
S(x)\not=\left\{e\right\}
for some point, it is nontrivial at every point by Lemma
13.13
. Since
a_{0}^{\pm 1}
-recurrence and dense
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-orbit are both residual properties, we may find some
x_{1}
such that
x_{1}
has a dense
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-orbit and for which
\operatorname{Lie}(S(x_{1}))
contains an element of an Oseledets space by Lemma
13.14
. But since
a_{*}S(x_{1})=S(a\cdot x_{1})
and Oseledets spaces are invariant under
a_{*}
, it follows that
\operatorname{Lie}(S(x))
contains that element for all
x
. This contradicts
(HA-2)
, the locally free property.∎

Proof of Theorem
13.2
.

We have just shown that
S(x)=\left\{e\right\}
,
\operatorname{Stab}_{G}(x)
is discrete. The group
\hat{G}
is Lie by Corollary
13.12
and contains the
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K
-action as a subgroup. The result follows.
∎

13.3.
Proof of Theorem
11.8

By Theorem
12.1
and Lemma
11.17
, any action satisfying the assumptions of Theorem
11.8
has pairwise constant cycle structure (recall Definition
13.1
). Therefore, Theorem
11.8
is a consequence of Theorem
13.2
.

14.
Smooth Partially Hyperbolic Actions

In this section, we verify that for the smooth abelian actions of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
satsifying assumptions as in Theorem
2.16
or
2.18
, the principal bundle extensions constructed in Section
10
are in fact genuinely higher-rank HAPHA actions with integral Lyapunov exponents and SRB measures. The bulk of the work needed for this is already done in Section
10
, in particular in Theorem
10.16
. Checking that these extensions have integral Lyapunov coefficients property (recall the paragraph following Definition
12.2
) is most involved and we do this in Section
14.1
. Then Theorem
11.8
gives a topological conjugacy to a homogeneous model. Verifying that the conjugating map is smooth is done in Section
14.2
.

Proposition 14.1
.

If
\mathbb{R}^{k}\curvearrowright X
is a
C^{\infty}
action as in Theorems
2.16
or
2.18
, then the
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K
-action on
\hat{X}
(that is, the Brin-Pesin compact extension of the action constructed in Section
10.4
where
K
is the fiber group of the extension) is a genuinely higher-rank HAPHA with integral Lyapunov coefficients and SRB measures satisfying
(HA-7)
(a) or
(HA-7)
(b), respectively.

Proof.

Property
(HA-1)
follows from the construction of the Brin-Pesin bundle (Proposition
F.10
).

For the property
(HA-2)
, the continuity of the
N^{\alpha}
actions follows from Theorem
10.16
, and the fact that
N^{\alpha}
is
\Delta
-harnessed (recall Definition
11.1
) follows from Theorem
10.16
and Lemma
9.9
.
The property
(HA-3)
is proved in Theorem
10.16
.

Property
(HA-4)
follows from the total recurrence of the base
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-action (which is due to volume preservation) and Lemma
F.7
.

For property
(HA-5)
, since each
N^{\alpha}
acts on
\hat{X}
in a well-defined manner, the free product
\mathcal{P}
also acts on
\hat{X}
in a well-defined way. The injectivity of the restriction of the evaluation maps to
N^{\chi_{1}}\times\cdots\times N^{\chi_{r}}
follows from the corresponding property on the base manifold
X
, as established in Proposition
5.4
, together with the transversality of the (lifted) coarse Lyapunov foliations with the fibers. We denote by
\hat{W}^{s}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{m}}(x)
the image stated in
(HA-5)
passing through
x
.

To verify Property
(HA-6)
, observe that any point
\hat{y}\in\hat{W}^{s}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{m}}(\hat{x})
can be connected to
\hat{x}
through an
\{N^{\chi_{1}},\dots,N^{\chi_{r}}\}
-path. By the definition of
\chi_{i}
and the discussion of lifted foliations in Proposition
F.4
, each
a_{j}
uniformly contracts all
N^{\chi_{i}}
-paths. Therefore, for any
\hat{y}\in W^{s}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{m}}(\hat{x})
, we have
d(a_{j}^{t}\hat{x},a_{j}^{t}\hat{y})\to 0
as
t\to\infty
.

Conversely, let
\hat{x},\hat{y}\in\hat{X}
cover
x,y\in X
, and assume that
d(a_{j}^{t}\hat{x},a_{j}^{t}\hat{y})\to 0
for all
1\leq j\leq m
. It follows immediately that
d(a_{j}^{t}x,a_{j}^{t}y)\to 0
, so
y\in W^{cs}(x)
. Since the
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-action is continuously Oseledets conformal (it satisfies property
(HOC)
(c) in Section
8.4
), it is isometric along
E^{c}
. In particular,
E^{c}
is integrable (see
[
11
]
) and there exists a unique
z\in W^{c}(y)\cap W^{s}(x)
. But since the dynamics along
W^{c}
is isometric and
d(a_{j}^{t}x,a_{j}^{t}y)\geq d(a_{j}^{t}z,a_{j}^{t}y)-d(a_{j}^{t}x,a_{j}^{t}z)\to d(z,y)
, it follows that
y=z
and
y\in W^{s}(x)
.
Then by Proposition
F.4
,
\hat{y}
lies in the lifted stable foliation of
X
. Since on the base manifold
W^{s}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{m}}(\hat{x})
is a product of
W^{\chi_{i}}
, the transversality of the lifted foliations implies that the lifted foliation of
W^{s}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{m}}(\hat{x})
is a product of
N^{\chi_{i}}
. This completes the verification of Property
(HA-6)
.

Property
(HA-7)
(a) holds for actions as in Theorem
2.18
as a direct consequence of the super accessibility assumption in Theorem
2.18
and the construction of the compact extension in Section 10. Property
(HA-7)
(b) holds for actions in Theorem
2.16
and it is a direct consequence of the local product structure of
s
,
u
,
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K
foliations for Anosov actions.

Property
(HA-8)
follows immediately from
(GHR)
, which was assumed (resp. established in Section
8
) for actions in Theorem
2.16
(resp. Theorem
2.18
).

For Property
(HA-9)
, we start by considering the
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-action invariant volume on
X
(for both Theorem
2.18
and
2.16
). Absolute continuity of unstable (and stable) foliations for every partially hyperbolic element of our action is a classical result due to Brin and Pesin
[
10
]
. Then we apply exactly the same argument as in the proof of the
[
72
, Proposition 4.22]
and argue by induction on dimension, i.e. we show by induction that
W^{\alpha}
is absolutely continuous in common unstable manifolds of
increasing dimension contained in
W^{u}_{a_{0}}(\supset W^{\alpha})
, where
a_{0}
is sufficiently close to
\ker\alpha
such that
\alpha(a_{0})>0
.

To construct
\mu
that satisfies
(HA-9)
, for any Borel
A\subset\hat{X}
take

\mu(A):=\int_{X}\int_{K}\mathbf{1}_{A}(x,y)d\nu_{x}(y)d\mathrm{vol}(x),

where we denote by
\nu_{x}
the Haar measure on the fiber of the compact extension at
y
. The measure
\mu
is obviously invariant under the lifted
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-action (defined in Theorem
10.16
) since the principal bundle structure is invariant.

Take an arbitrary measurable partition
\mathcal{P}_{X}
of
\mathrm{vol}
of
X
which is subordinate to
W^{\alpha}
(without loss of generality we can assume the diameter of each atom of
\mathcal{P}_{X}
is much smaller then the size of local trivialization charts of the fiber bundle). It lifts naturally to a measurable partition
\mathcal{P}_{\hat{X}}
of
\hat{X}
which is subordinate to the lifted foliation
\hat{W}^{\alpha}
. For each atom
P
, the local holonomy of
\hat{W}^{\alpha}
induces a local product structure of

\pi^{-1}(P)\cong P\times K.

We claim that for
\mathrm{vol}
-almost every atom
P
of
\mathcal{P}_{X}
, the conditional measure of
\mu
on
\pi^{-1}(P)
has the product form with respect to this local product structure

\mu|_{\pi^{-1}(P)}\cong\mathrm{vol}|_{P}\times\nu.

By the Avila-Viana invariance principle
[
3
, Corollary 4.3]
, since the lifted
\mathbb{R}^{k}
-action is isometric along fibers and therefore has
0
-Lyapunov exponents everywhere along fibers, we know
\mu
is
\hat{W}^{\alpha}
holonomy-invariant. This implies the product structure: it is a consequece of a general fact that if a measure
\mu
on a product space
X\times Y
has the same conditional measure
d\mu_{x}(\cdot),\cdot\in Y
for almost every
x\in X
, then
\mu
is a product of
\mathrm{Proj}^{X}(\mu)
with this invariant conditional measure
\mu_{x}
. In particular for
\mu|_{\pi^{-1}(P)}
-almost every local leaf
\hat{W}^{\alpha}(\hat{x})
,

(14.1)

\pi_{\ast}(\mu|_{\hat{W}^{\alpha}(\hat{x})})=\mathrm{vol}|_{W^{\alpha}(x)}\text{ where }\pi(\hat{x})=x.

By our construction of
\hat{X}
and
\hat{W}
we know
\pi
interwines the
N^{\alpha}
action on each leaf
\hat{W}^{\alpha}(\hat{x})
with a
C^{1}

N^{\alpha}
action along
W^{\alpha}(x)
, hence the absolute continuity of
\mathrm{vol}
along
W^{\alpha}(x)
combined with (
14.1
) implies
(HA-9)
.

Since the proof of integral Lyapunov coefficients is more involved, we prove it in
Lemma
14.4
of the next subsection.

∎

14.1.
Integral Lyapunov coefficients

Recall that a
common stable foliation
is a foliation
W
of
X
whose leaves are given by
\bigcap_{k=1}^{n}W^{s}_{a_{k}}(x)
for some collection
a_{1},\dots,a_{k}
of partially hyperbolic elements of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
. Common stable foliations are Hölder foliations with smooth leaves (see
[
72
, Lemma 4.5]
).

Consider the compact extension
\hat{X}
over
X
constructed in Section
10.4
. For any common stable foliation
\bigcap_{k=1}^{n}W^{s}_{a_{k}}(x)
of
X
,
the proof of Proposition
14.1

shows that it can be lifted to a topological foliation
\hat{W}^{s}_{(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})}
on
\hat{X}
satisfying
(HA-5)
and
(HA-6)
. Moreover, the projection
\pi:\hat{X}\to X
, when restricted to any leaf
\hat{W}^{s}_{(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})}
of
\hat{X}
, is a homeomorphism onto a common stable leaf
\bigcap_{k=1}^{n}W^{s}_{a_{k}}(x)
of
X
. For any
x\in X
, choosing an arbitrary
\hat{x}\in\hat{X}
such that
\pi(\hat{x})=x
, there exists a unique leaf
\hat{W}^{s}_{(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})}(\hat{x})
passing through
\hat{x}
that projects to
\bigcap_{k=1}^{n}W^{s}_{a_{k}}(x)
(see also Appendix
F
).

By Proposition
14.1
, for any
x\in X
and any lift
\hat{x}\in\hat{X}
of
x
, the group
N^{\alpha}
acts freely on the leaf
\hat{W}^{s}_{(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})}(\hat{x})
provided that
\alpha(a_{k})<0
for all
k
. This action naturally induces (via
\pi
) a free action of
N^{\alpha}
on
\bigcap_{k=1}^{n}W^{s}_{a_{k}}(x)
. However, since the choice of
\hat{x}
is not unique, there is no canonical way to define an
N^{\alpha}
action on
\bigcap_{k=1}^{n}W^{s}_{a_{k}}(x)
. In what follows, unless otherwise specified, we will implicitly choose an arbitrary
\hat{x}
and define the corresponding
N^{\alpha}
action on a common stable foliation (or the action by a subgroup of
N^{\alpha}
on a subfoliation of a common stable foliation). Consequently, we can also discuss the geometric commutator of the
N^{\alpha}
and
N^{\beta}
actions on
\bigcap_{k=1}^{n}W^{s}_{a_{k}}(x)
, provided that
\alpha(a_{k}),\beta(a_{k})<0
for all
k
. This commutator is well-defined once we fix an arbitrary lift
\hat{x}
.

Lemma 14.2
.

Consider a common stable foliation
W
, which is the sum of coarse Lyapunov distributions
E_{\alpha_{1}},\dots,E_{\alpha_{n}}
. Let
(\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{n})
be an arbitrary ordering of
\{\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n}\}
.
Then the map

\phi_{x}:\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}E_{\beta_{i}}\to W(x)\subset X

defined by

\phi_{x}(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})=\exp(u_{1})\exp(u_{2})\dots\exp(u_{n})\cdot x

is surjective from a neighborhood of 0 in
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}E_{\beta_{i}}
onto a neighborhood of
x
in
W(x)
.

Here, we identify
E_{\beta_{i}}
with
\text{Lie}(N^{\beta_{i}})
, and
\exp
denotes the exponential map from
\text{Lie}(N^{\beta_{i}})
to
N^{\beta_{i}}
. Although the actions of the nilpotent groups on
W(x)
depend on the choice of the lifting
\hat{x}
of
x
, and the identification between
E_{\beta_{i}}
and
\text{Lie}(N^{\beta_{i}})
is also non-canonical, Lemma
14.2
holds for any such identification and any lifting. This result follows directly from
[
69
, Lemma 3.2]
, which builds on ideas from
[
54
]
, particularly Corollary 4.5 in
[
54
]
.
We do not offer a complete proof, but summarize the strategy: note that if the actions of the nilpotent groups were smooth, this would be true from the inverse function theorem. One may then use smooth approximations and show that in the limit the original action is recovered, and the onto property persists by topological degree arguments.

Lemma 14.3
.

Let
\mathbb{R}^{k}\curvearrowright X
be as in Theorem
2.16
or
2.18
and
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K\curvearrowright\hat{X}
be the action constructed in Section
10.4
on the compact extension
\hat{X}
.
If for any non-proportional
\alpha,\beta\in\Delta
, any
c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha,c_{j}^{\beta}\beta\in\Omega
, we have that

E_{|c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha,c_{j}^{\beta}\beta|}:=\bigoplus_{c_{k}^{\alpha}\geq c_{i}^{\alpha}}E^{c_{k}^{\alpha}\alpha}\oplus\bigoplus_{c_{l}^{\beta}\geq c_{j}^{\beta}}E^{c_{l}^{\beta}\beta}\oplus\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\Sigma(\alpha,\beta)}\left(\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{m}^{\gamma}\gamma=\sigma c^{\alpha}_{i}\alpha+\tau c^{\beta}_{j}\beta\\
\sigma\geq 1\text{ or }\tau\geq 1\end{subarray}}E^{c_{m}^{\gamma}\gamma}\right)

on
TX
is topologically integrable, then the action
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K\curvearrowright\hat{X}
has integral Lyapunov coefficients.

Proof.

For
\gamma\in\Sigma(\alpha,\beta)
denote
A^{\prime}_{\gamma}=\{c_{m}^{\gamma}\gamma:c_{m}^{\gamma}\gamma=\sigma c^{\alpha}_{i}\alpha+\tau c^{\beta}_{j}\beta\mbox{ with }\sigma\geq 1\text{ or }\tau\geq 1\}
.

Recall that for any
x\in X,u\in E^{c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha}(x),v\in E^{c_{j}^{\beta}\beta}(x)
we could define the geometric commutator of
u,v
within the common stable leaf
W^{|\alpha,\beta|}(x):=\cap_{\alpha(a),\beta(a)<0}W^{s}_{a}(x)
in Definition
11.12
, which may depend on the choice of the lift
\hat{x}
of
x
. By the integrability of
E_{|c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha,c_{j}^{\beta}\beta|}
and Lemma
F.8
, its integral foliation
W^{|c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha,c_{j}^{\beta}\beta|}
can be lifted to a topological foliation
\hat{W}^{|c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha,c_{j}^{\beta}\beta|}
which is closed under the action by

\hat{N}^{c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha}:=\exp\left(\bigoplus_{c_{k}^{\alpha}\geq c_{i}^{\alpha}}E^{c_{k}^{\alpha}\alpha}\right),\quad\hat{N}^{c_{j}^{\beta}\beta}:=\exp\left(\bigoplus_{c_{l}^{\beta}\geq c_{j}^{\beta}}E^{c_{l}^{\beta}\beta}\right)

and

\hat{N}^{\gamma,c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha,c_{i}^{\beta}\beta}:=\exp\left(\bigoplus_{A^{\prime}_{\gamma}}E^{c_{m}^{\gamma}\gamma}\right)

for
\gamma\in\Sigma(\alpha,\beta)
. Here we identify these distributions as subalgebras of
N^{\alpha},N^{\beta}
and
N^{(\alpha,\beta)}
respectively.

We claim that if
\Sigma(\alpha,\beta)=\left\{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{\ell}\right\}
is a circular ordering, it suffices to show that the map

\phi_{\hat{x}}:\hat{N}^{c_{j}\beta}\times\prod_{i=\#\Sigma(\alpha,\beta)}^{\ell}\hat{N}^{\gamma_{i},c_{i}\alpha,c_{j}\beta}\times\hat{N}^{c_{i}\alpha}\to W^{|c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha,c_{j}^{\beta}\beta|}(\hat{x})

defined by
\phi(u,v_{1},\dots,v_{\ell},w)=\exp(u)\exp(v_{1})\dots\exp(v_{\ell})\exp(w)\hat{x}
is a local homeomorphism at 0 for every
\hat{x}
. Indeed, if this is the case, then following Definitions
11.12
and
12.2
for commutators between
\hat{N}^{c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha}\subset N^{\alpha}
and
\hat{N}^{c_{j}^{\beta}\beta}\subset N^{\beta}
, we observe that the terms
\rho^{\gamma}_{\alpha,\beta}(\exp(u),\exp(w),\hat{x})
must take values in the groups
\hat{N}^{\gamma,c_{i}\alpha,c_{j}\beta}\subset N^{\gamma}
, since the geometric commutators are unique. That is, the action must have integral Lyapunov coefficients.

So we must show that
\phi
is a (local) homeomorphism onto its image. This follows the same proof scheme as Proposition
5.4
, where the arguments take place within
W^{|c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha,c_{j}^{\beta}\beta|}(x)
on the base manifold, and lifting the uniqueness of the path to the Brin-Pesin fibration.∎

Lemma 14.4
.

If
\mathbb{R}^{k}\curvearrowright X
is a
C^{2}
action as in Theorem
2.16
or Theorem
2.18
, then
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K
-action on
\hat{X}
constructed in Section
10.4
has integral Lyapunov coefficients.

Proof.

By Lemma
14.3
it suffices to show on
X
that for any non-proportional
\alpha,\beta\in\Delta
, and any
c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha
in the class of
\alpha
, any
c_{j}^{\beta}\beta
in the class of
\beta
,
the distrbution
E_{|c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha,c_{j}^{\beta}\beta|}
defined in Lemma
14.3
of
TX
is topologically integrable.

Fix Lyapunov functionals
c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha\in[\alpha]
and
c_{j}^{\beta}\beta\in[\beta]
. Recall the definition
12.3
of
\Omega(\alpha,\beta)
. Then we divide

\Omega(\alpha,\beta)\cup\{c_{k}^{\alpha}\alpha,k=1,\dots\}\cup\{c_{\ell}^{\beta}\beta,\ell=1,\dots\}=A\cup B,

where
A=\left\{\sigma\,c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha+\tau\,c_{j}^{\beta}\beta:\sigma\geq 1\right\}\cap\Omega
and
B=\left\{\sigma\,c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha+\tau\,c_{j}^{\beta}\beta:\sigma<1\right\}\cap\Omega
. Let

B^{\prime}=\left\{\sigma\,c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha+\tau\,c_{j}^{\beta}\beta:\sigma<1,\tau\geq 1\right\}\cap\Omega\subset B.

Give the coarse Lyapunov exponents in
\Sigma(\alpha,\beta)\cup\{\alpha,\beta\}
a circular ordering
\left\{\alpha=\chi_{1},\dots,\chi_{n}=\beta\right\}
. We will show that if
c_{p}^{\chi_{k}}\chi_{k},c_{q}^{\chi_{\ell}}\chi_{\ell}\in A\cup B^{\prime}
, then
[c_{p}^{\chi_{k}}\chi_{k},c_{q}^{\chi_{\ell}}\chi_{\ell}]\subset A\cup B^{\prime}
by induction on
\left|k-\ell\right|
. Then the result follows since
\alpha=\chi_{1}
and
\beta=\chi_{n}
.

The base case is trivial: if
\left|k-\ell\right|=1
, then
\chi_{k}
and
\chi_{\ell}
are adjacent in the circular ordering, and therefore
N^{\chi_{k}}
and
N^{\chi_{\ell}}
commute. We now try to commute
c_{p}^{\chi_{k}}\chi_{k},c_{q}^{\chi_{\ell}}\chi_{\ell}\in A\cup B^{\prime}
,
\left|k-\ell\right|>1
. We break into cases based on whether each weight lies in
A
or
B^{\prime}
. The easiest occurs when both belong to
A
or both belong to
B^{\prime}
, so we cover these first.

Recall that
W=W^{\left|\alpha,\beta\right|}
is the foliation whose leaves are tangent to
\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n}E_{\chi_{k}}
, which is a common stable manifold. Notice that choosing
a\in\ker\beta
, and perturbing by a very small amount will yield an element
a^{\prime}
close to
a
for which
\left(\bigoplus_{\chi\in A}E^{\chi}\right)\oplus\left(\bigoplus_{\chi\in B}E^{\chi}\right)
is a dominated splitting of
TW
. Therefore,
\bigoplus_{c_{p}^{\chi_{k}}\chi_{k}\in A}E^{c_{p}^{\chi_{k}}\chi_{k}}
is tangent to a foliation
W^{A}
.

Assuming we have fixed
c_{i}^{\alpha}\alpha
and
c_{j}^{\beta}\beta
, if
\chi\in\Sigma(\alpha,\beta)
, let
\hat{N}^{\chi}
be the nilpotent group tangent to the subalgebra

\hat{\mathfrak{n}}_{\chi}=\bigoplus_{c_{p}^{\chi}\chi\in A}E^{c_{p}^{\chi}\chi}.

This is a (possibly trivial) subalgebra since each
a\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
acts by an automorphism of
N^{\chi}
, so by standard Lie theory,
[E^{c_{p}^{\chi}\chi},E^{c_{q}^{\chi}\chi}]\subset E^{(c_{p}^{\chi}+c_{q}^{\chi})\chi}
.
Notice that each leaf
W^{A}
has local
C^{0}
surjections given by the maps from
\hat{N}^{\chi_{1}}\times\hat{N}^{\chi_{2}}\times\cdots\times\hat{N}^{\chi_{n}}\to W^{A}(x)
by

(14.2)

(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})\mapsto u_{1}\cdot u_{2}\cdot\dots\cdot u_{n}\cdot x.

The proof of this claim uses Lemma
14.2
. See also Lemma 5.11 of
[
72
]
. Essentially it follows from transversality of the distributions, the difficulty being that they are only Hölder. Indeed, these show that (
14.2
) gives coordinates in a neighborhood near 0 and
x
, then use the expanding dynamics to show they are global coordinates. In particular, if both
c_{p}^{\chi_{k}}\chi_{k}
and
c_{q}^{\chi_{\ell}}\chi_{\ell}\in A
,
[c_{p}^{\chi_{k}}\chi_{k},c_{q}^{\chi_{\ell}}\chi_{\ell}]\subset A
. Similarly, if both
c_{p}^{\chi_{k}}\chi_{k}
and
c_{q}^{\chi_{\ell}}\chi_{\ell}
belong to
B^{\prime}
, then they both belong to
C=\left\{u\alpha+v\beta:v\geq 1\right\}\cap\Omega
. By identical arguments, choosing a perturbation of
b\in\ker\alpha
, gives
[B^{\prime},B^{\prime}]\subset[C,C]\subset C\subset A\cup B^{\prime}
.

We now consider the case when
c_{p}^{\chi_{k}}\chi_{k}\in B^{\prime}
and
c_{q}^{\chi_{\ell}}\chi_{\ell}\in A
with
\left|k-\ell\right|>1
(the case when
c_{p}^{\chi_{k}}\chi_{k}\in A
and
c_{q}^{\chi_{\ell}}\chi_{\ell}\in B^{\prime}
follows from a symmetric argument). Let
x\in X
,
u\in E^{c_{p}^{\chi_{k}}\chi_{k}}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(N^{\chi_{k}})
and
v\in E^{c_{q}^{\chi_{\ell}}\chi_{\ell}}\subset\operatorname{Lie}(N^{\chi_{\ell}})
. We construct points related to a geometric commutator in the following way

\begin{array}[]{ccc}y=e(u)\cdot x&&x^{\prime}=e(v)\cdot x\\
y^{\prime}=e(v)\cdot y&&w=e(u)\cdot x^{\prime}\end{array}

Notice that
y^{\prime}=[e(-v),e(-u)]\cdot w
(see Figure
3
).

Let
\Sigma_{k\ell}:=\left\{\chi_{k+1},\dots,\chi_{\ell-1}\right\}
be the set of coarse functionals strictly between
\chi_{k}
and
\chi_{\ell}
. Each coarse functional of
\Sigma_{k\ell}
splits into Lyapunov functional, let
\Omega_{k\ell}
denote the set of Lyapunov functionals proportional to a coarse exponent of
\Sigma_{k\ell}
. Now write
\Omega_{k\ell}
as

\Omega_{k\ell}=\left\{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{m_{1}},\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{m_{2}},\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{m_{3}}\right\},

where
\left\{\gamma_{\bullet}\right\}
,
\left\{\delta_{\bullet}\right\}
and
\left\{\epsilon_{\bullet}\right\}
are the exponents of
A\cap\Omega_{k\ell}
,
(B\setminus B^{\prime})\cap\Omega_{k\ell}
and
B^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{k\ell}
, respectively, with each subset listed in an order to be clarified later.

We assume
y
and
w
are sufficiently close, to be determined later (if we show it for sufficiently small
u,v
, we may use the dynamics of
\mathbb{R}^{k}\times K\curvearrowright\hat{X}
projects to
\mathbb{R}^{k}\curvearrowright X
and Theorem
10.16
to conclude it for arbitrary
u,v
).

Notice that the distribution
\bigoplus_{s=k+1}^{\ell-1}E_{\chi_{s}}
is uniquely integrable to a foliation
W^{k\ell}
with
C^{2}
leaves since it is the intersection of stable manifolds for the action. Since
y^{\prime}=[e(-v),e(-u)]\cdot w
,
y^{\prime}\in W^{k\ell}(w)
by Lemma
11.10
. Therefore, by Lemma
14.2
applied to the splitting
T\mathcal{W}^{k\ell}
into the bundles
E^{\gamma_{s}}
,
E^{\delta_{s}}
and
E^{\epsilon_{s}}
, there exists a path moving from
w
to
y^{\prime}
which first moves along curves of the form
\exp(w)
, where
w\in E^{\epsilon_{s}}
, to arrive at a point
p
. Then similarly along exponential images of
w\in E^{\delta_{s}}
to arrive at a point
q
from
p
. Finally, we move along the exponential images of vectors in
E^{\gamma_{s}}
to connect
q
to
y^{\prime}
. In this way,
p
is obtained from
w
after moving along curves tangent to
B^{\prime}
, and
q
is obtained from
p
by moving along curves tangent to
B\setminus B^{\prime}
. Then
q
is also connected to
y^{\prime}
via curves tangent to
A
. See Figure
3
.

x
e(u)

e(v)
x^{\prime}
e(v)
